TITLE: Factorwise Weighted-Polar Accounting and a Sharp Whole-Relation Obstruction
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: For every prime p and integer 101≤d<p, let B∈F_p[X,Y,Z] be primitive in Z, squarefree, of positive Z-degree, and of (1,1,d)-weighted degree δ<p. Factor B=c∏C_i, put δ_i=wdeg(C_i), r_i=deg_Z(C_i), γ_i=wdeg(C_{i,Z}), and Xi_i=(r_i−1)δ_i+r_iγ_i−dr_i(r_i−1). For any disjoint assignment of labeled affine graph lines T=⊔T_i satisfying C_i(L(t),P_L(t))≡0, at most Xi_i lines of T_i are identically C_{i,Z}-ramified, every other assigned graph has total polar intersection multiplicity at most γ_i, and the normalized accepted-incidence cleanup loss is at most sum_i min{|T_i|,Xi_i}/[p(p+1)]+sum_i |T_i|γ_i/[p²(p+1)]. A connected cleaned factor class of minimum degree k>δ_i with 1/sqrt(p+1)≤k/(4p) yields one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with global agreement at least k/(4p). Conversely, for every 2d≤D<p there is an explicit legal table and primitive squarefree relation of weighted degree D for which the whole-relation polar loss is exactly (D−d)/(p+1)=τ(D−d)/p, with every intersection simple; for p≥8d this occurs at ε=Theta((d/p)^(1/3)). The construction has a factor Z with derivative one and a degree-zero global decoder, so it is a proof-architecture obstruction rather than a soundness counterexample.
GLOBAL: A factorwise weighted-resultant and local-intersection-multiplicity lemma is proved. It removes cross-factor collisions from the vertical ramification charge and conditionally yields one global polynomial on any large cleaned factor component. An explicit legal table shows that the unfactored per-line polar bound D−d, including multiplicity accounting, is exactly sharp at the cubic parameter scale. The example is reducible and has a lower-weight factor decoder, so it does not prove sharpness for KTZ's minimum interpolant and does not improve or refute the unconditional 1/3 benchmark.
STEPS:
  P1 [proved] Every irreducible factor C_i depends on Z and satisfies gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1.
  P2 [proved] The weighted resultant has base degree at most su+rv−drs.
  P3 [proved] The assigned factor C_i has at most Xi_i identically ramified graph lines, counted with resultant multiplicity.
  P4 [proved] Every nonexceptional assigned graph has total local polar intersection multiplicity at most γ_i.
  P5 [proved] The factorwise ramified-incidence measure obeys the exact and coarse bounds in Theorem A.
  P6 [conditional] A connected cleaned factor class satisfying the stated degree and mixing hypotheses yields one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with agreement at least k/(4p).
  P7 [proved] The explicit table in Theorem C has local agreement (D−d)/(p+1).
  P8 [proved] The whole-relation polar loss equals τ(D−d)/p exactly, whereas the factorwise loss is zero.
  P9 [proved] The sharpness construction realizes the cubic parameter balance.
  P10 [refuted] The sharpness construction forces KTZ's minimum-weight interpolant to incur the whole-relation polar loss.
  P11 [refuted] Inseparability or concentrated good directions refute the factorwise theorem in the campaign regime.
OBSTRUCTIONS:
Whole-polynomial B_Z charges cross-factor collisions even when the factor carrying the graph is vertically simple.
The per-line whole-polar bound D−d is exactly sharp, with multiplicity-one roots, and its normalized loss can equal all local agreement at the cubic scale.
Counting singular lifted points instead of incidences fails under p-fold concurrency.
No current argument guarantees that a factor class of small weighted polar degree retains minimum degree Omega(εp).
Even perfect factorwise cleanup leaves KTZ's earlier identity-extension requirement D=O(εp).
The saturation example is not minimum-weight; proving a minimum-interpolant obstruction remains open.
NEXT:
Prove a factor-consistency dichotomy: either one low-weight factor class retains minimum degree Omega(εp), or cross-factor intersections directly expose a degree-d decoder.
Determine whether a minimum-weight KTZ interpolant can realize a Theta(D−d) polar loss on a diffuse, nondecodable carrier; the present saturation example cannot.

TITLE: Sharp Incidence-Only Losses in Bivariate Prime-Field Line–Point Agreement
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: Let p be prime, let 101≤d<p, fix m=2, and use uniform affine point-line sampling. There exist f:F_p²→F_p and degree-exactly-d line labels such that ε=d/p, every affine line has exactly d accepted points, every point has accepted degree d or d+1, exactly pd points have degree d+1, and every line supports acceptance. For every θ∈(0,1], the minimum total nonnegative line weight that gives every accepted incidence θ capped coverage through other accepted lines lies between θp(p+1)/(d+1) and θp(p+1)/(d−1); hence every integral such family has size Ω(p/ε). Nevertheless Q=0 has global agreement one. Moreover, for every valid table of agreement ε, the probability Γ₂ that two uniformly chosen distinct lines through a uniform point both accept there satisfies Γ₂=ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var_x(deg_acc(x))/[p(p+1)], and the lower bound is attained by accepting exactly k direction classes, ε=k/(p+1).
GLOBAL: No unconditional exponent beyond 1/3 is proved. The new result sharply rules out two incidence-blind improvements: compatible two-line collisions can be only Θ(ε²), and a universal family reaching a constant fraction of every accepted line can require Θ(p/ε) fractional weight even on an exactly almost-regular, fully supported, legal table. Because the extremal tables have the obvious decoder Q=0, the conclusion is a rigorous sparsify-or-decode obstruction, not a counterexample to soundness. KTZ already uses the exact affine Vinh estimate; improving toward exponent 1/2 must attack covering/interpolation or decode the obstructing case, while any exponent above 1/2 also needs a replacement for Vinh-only component recovery in low-d regimes.
STEPS:
  P1 [proved] The affine point-line incidence graph admits a p+1-color decomposition in which every point sees all colors and each line misses one color.
  P2 [proved] There is a spanning affine incidence subgraph with every line of degree d and every point of degree d or d+1.
  P3 [proved] The regular incidence subgraph is realized by a legal degree-d line-versus-point table.
  P4 [proved] Every fractional family reaching a θ-fraction of every line through other accepted lines has weight at least θp(p+1)/(d+1).
  P5 [proved] The other-line fractional-cover lower bound is tight within factor (d+1)/(d−1).
  P6 [proved] The ordered distinct-line joint-acceptance probability equals ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var(a_x)/[p(p+1)].
  P7 [proved] The collision lower bound is attained by concentrated good directions for every ε=k/(p+1).
  P8 [proved] The KTZ cubic threshold is reconstructed from covering, interpolation, and root supply.
  P9 [proved] Vinh-only component recovery has a method barrier at exponent 1/2 in the legal lower-boundary regime.
  P10 [proved] The quoted stronger prime-field incidence bounds do not supply linear component recovery within their own characteristic ranges.
  P11 [refuted] Acceptance density alone always yields either more than quadratic collision energy or an o(p/ε)-weight universal reaching family.
OBSTRUCTIONS:
The exact two-line compatibility energy can be only ε²−O(ε/p); no incidence convexity argument can force a better power from ε alone.
A universal fractional family reaching a constant fraction of every accepted line can require Θ(p/ε) total weight, even on a fully supported, exactly almost-regular legal table.
The point and line projections of the regular table are the entire affine plane and all affine lines. Vinh's main term is exact on these projections, so unweighted incidence bounds discard the acceptance information.
Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 3 requires comparable distinct sets and a characteristic-size condition; the component-size exponent it supplies becomes linear only outside that range.
Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 4 additionally requires a Cartesian point set and likewise has no parameter overlap where its consequence supplies linear recovery.
For fixed legal d and a candidate soundness exponent above 1/2, the KTZ/Vinh mixing requirement ε=Ω(p^−1/2) fails.
The extremal incidence examples are easy to decode, so none refutes the soundness target; they force a dichotomy rather than establish impossibility.
NEXT:
Prove a sparsify-or-decode dichotomy that recognizes regular or colored easy carriers before invoking a universal reaching family.
Develop incidence estimates for the lifted compatibility relation (point value together with line polynomial), rather than for its base point and line projections.
Replace Vinh-only component recovery for candidate exponents α>1/2, especially at the legal lower boundary d=101.
Investigate whether large joint dependencies among weighted interpolation constraints force a low-weight irreducible factor or an immediate global decoder.
Determine whether the regular Hall-factor carrier can be made connected while preserving the Θ(p/ε) other-line fractional cover cost; connectivity is not proved here.
Seek a nonuniform, algebra-aware selector whose cost is charged to undecoded components rather than to every accepted line simultaneously.

/bin/zsh -lc "jq -r '.lemmas[] | select(.source_job_id==\"researcher-0002\" and (.source_step_id==\"P5\" or .source_step_id==\"P6\" or .source_step_id==\"P7\")) | \"ID \" + .id + \"\\n\" + .statement_markdown + \"\\nPROOF\\n\" + .proof_markdown + \"\\n\"' research_state/campaign-10-ultra/lemma_book/lemma-book.json" in /Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point

ID researcher-0002:P5.1
Under the hypotheses and notation of P4, write

$$
A=\sum_j a_j(X,Y)Z^j,
\qquad
H(X,Y)=\gcd_j a_j(X,Y),
\qquad
B=A/H.
$$

Then $B$ is squarefree, has total degree less than $p$, and satisfies

$$
B(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in T$ such that $H(L(s))\not\equiv0$. There exists a formal directional derivative $\mathcal{D}$ such that

$$
\gcd(B,\mathcal{D}B)=1.
$$

Define

$$
T_{\mathrm{triv}}=\{L\in T:H(L(s))\equiv0\}
$$

and

$$
T_{\mathrm{deg}}=
\left\{L\in T\setminus T_{\mathrm{triv}}:(\mathcal{D}B)(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
|T_{\mathrm{triv}}|\le D,
\qquad
|T_{\mathrm{deg}}|\le D(D-1),
$$

and every $L\in T\setminus(T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}})$ satisfies

$$
\left|\{x\in N_G(L):(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\}\right|\le D.
$$

Consequently, for

$$
E_{\mathrm{exc}}=
\left\{(x,L)\in E:L\in T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}}
\text{ or }(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\right\},
$$

one has

$$
|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|\le pD^2+Dt.
$$
PROOF
At most $D$ affine lines can make $H(L(s))$ vanish identically, because each such line contributes a distinct linear factor of $H$ and $\deg H\le D$. On any other line, the identity from P4 reads

$$
H(L(s))B(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0.
$$

Since $\mathbb{F}_p[s]$ is an integral domain and $H(L(s))$ is nonzero, exact polynomial division gives $B(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0$.

The quotient $B$ is squarefree and has degree below $p$. Since $\mathbb{F}_p$ is perfect, an irreducible factor whose three formal partial derivatives all vanish would be a nontrivial power with exponent $p$, which is impossible here. KTZ Lemma 2.4 therefore supplies a formal directional derivative $\mathcal{D}$ satisfying $\gcd(B,\mathcal{D}B)=1$.

KTZ Lemma 2.6 applies the relevant resultant to bound by $D(D-1)$ the lines on whose polynomial graphs both $B$ and $\mathcal{D}B$ vanish identically. On every other nontrivial line, the restricted derivative is a nonzero univariate polynomial of degree at most $D$, so it has at most $D$ accepted zeros. The trivial and degenerate lines contribute at most

$$
pD+pD(D-1)=pD^2
$$

edges, and all remaining lines contribute at most $Dt$. Thus $|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|\le pD^2+Dt$. The argument uses a resultant rather than a discriminant.

ID researcher-0002:P6.1
Under the hypotheses and notation of P5,

$$
\frac{|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|}{|E|}
\le\frac{D}{k}+\frac{16384dp}{k^2}
<\frac1{4000}.
$$

Set

$$
k_1=\left\lfloor\frac{k}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Starting from $(X,T,E\setminus E_{\mathrm{exc}})$ and iteratively deleting every vertex whose current degree is less than $k_1$ removes fewer than

$$
k_1(|X|+t)
\le2\frac{k_1}{k}|E|
\le\frac{|E|}{50}
$$

additional edges and leaves a nonempty subgraph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $k_1$.
PROOF
Since every line vertex of $G$ has degree at least $k$,

$$
|E|\ge kt.
$$

Using P5 and the bound $D^2\le16384dt/k$ from P3 gives

$$
\frac{|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|}{|E|}
\le\frac{pD^2+Dt}{|E|}
\le\frac{D}{k}+\frac{16384dp}{k^2}
<\frac1{4000}.
$$

During peeling, each removed vertex accounts for fewer than $k_1$ edges present at its removal. The original minimum-degree condition on both vertex classes gives

$$
k(|X|+t)\le2|E|.
$$

Thus peeling removes fewer than

$$
k_1(|X|+t)
\le2\frac{k_1}{k}|E|
\le\frac{|E|}{50}
$$

additional edges. Together with the exceptional-edge bound, this is less than $|E|$, so at least one edge remains. The remaining graph has minimum degree at least $k_1$ by construction. It need not remain connected and may split into several components.

ID researcher-0002:P7.1
Let $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ be the surviving graph from P6, and let $B$ and $\mathcal{D}$ be as in P5. Then every $x\in X_1$ satisfies

$$
B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$
PROOF
The numerical hypotheses give $k_1\ge2$. Fix $x\in X_1$ and choose two distinct incident lines $L_1,L_2\in T_1$. Their direction vectors $v_1,v_2$ are linearly independent, since two distinct affine lines through one point have distinct directions in $\mathbb{F}_p^2$.

Choose parametrizations $L_i(s)=x+sv_i$. Differentiating the formal identities

$$
B(L_i(s),P_{L_i}(s))\equiv0
$$

at $s=0$ gives

$$
v_{i,X}B_X(x,f(x))+v_{i,Y}B_Y(x,f(x))
+B_Z(x,f(x))P_{L_i}'(0)=0
$$

for $i\in\{1,2\}$. If $B_Z(x,f(x))=0$, the independence of $v_1$ and $v_2$ forces

$$
B_X(x,f(x))=B_Y(x,f(x))=0.
$$

All three formal partial derivatives would then vanish at $(x,f(x))$, implying $(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0$. This contradicts the deletion rule defining $G_1$. Hence $B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$. No derivative of a line polynomial is divided by in this argument.

E2E-14 [definition/candidate] deps={E2E-02}

=== incidence-engine / round 1 ===
SUMMARY The exact corpus-mapped component chain gives a conditional exponent-$1/2$ decoder, while component support and universal cover arguments show that the same cardinality-based route cannot reach exponent $1-o(1)$. The proposed critical path replaces small $|R|$ by low labeled-constraint rank: preserve $\Omega_\eta(\epsilon)$ accepted mass and obtain a relation of weighted degree $D=O_\eta(d)$. Then cleanup costs only $O_\eta(d/p)$, simple roots split the graph among $O_\eta(1)$ factors, and one factor can yield one global polynomial with $\Omega_\eta(\epsilon)$ agreement. The main open incidence node is the low-weight sparsify-or-decode dichotomy; factor linearization also needs a fresh exact proof audit.
CRITICAL IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH -> IE-L-POPULAR-CORE -> IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY -> IE-L-RANK-INTERP -> IE-L-RANK-EXTEND -> IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN -> IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE -> IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT -> IE-R-CLOSE -> IE-GOAL
IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH [definition/candidate] deps={}
  Let $\mathcal L_p$ be the $p(p+1)$ affine lines of $\mathbb F_p^2$ and let $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(x,L):x\in L\text{ and }P_L(x)=f(x)\}.$$ Put $\epsilon=|E_{\rm acc}|/[p^2(p+1)]$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$ define $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\quad \mu(G)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\quad \tau(G)=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},\quad k(G)=\operatorname{mindeg}(G).$$
  NOTES All measures use the uniform affine point-line incidence normalization.
IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate] deps={IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH}
  For $d\le D<p$, let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},\qquad N_d(D)=\dim V_D,$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ set $$N_G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E\},$$ $$\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)=\{x\in N_G(L):\exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ (x,M)\in E\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
  NOTES This replaces cardinality control of $R$ by the actual rank of its labeled graph constraints.

ID researcher-0007:P1.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, and write

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i,
\qquad c\in\mathbb F_p^\times,
$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible polynomials. For

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\qquad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
$$

every $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ satisfies

$$
r_i\ge1
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1.
$$
PROOF
If some $C_i$ were independent of $Z$, then $C_i\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ would divide every coefficient of $B$ viewed as a polynomial in $Z$, contradicting primitivity. Hence $r_i>0$.

The weighted-degree bound gives

$$
d r_i\le \delta_i\le \delta<p,
$$

so $1\le r_i<p$. The derivative of the leading $Z$-term is therefore nonzero, and

$$
C_{i,Z}\ne0,
\qquad
\deg_Z(C_{i,Z})=r_i-1<r_i.
$$

Since $C_i$ is irreducible, any nonconstant common divisor of $C_i$ and $C_{i,Z}$ would be associated to $C_i$, which is impossible because $C_{i,Z}$ has smaller $Z$-degree. Thus $\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1$. Absolute irreducibility is not used.
STATUS proved

ID researcher-0007:P5.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, with

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i.
$$

For each $i$, put

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\quad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
\quad
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T=\bigsqcup_i\mathcal T_i$ be a disjoint assignment of distinct affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$, satisfying

$$
C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\qquad(L\in\mathcal T_i).
$$

For each $i$, let

$$
e_i=
\left|
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
\right|.
$$

For a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, let $i(L)=i$ when $L\in\mathcal T_i$, and define

$$
\mathcal R=
\left\{(L,t):
\begin{array}{l}
L\in\mathcal T,\ t\in\mathbb F_p,\\
P_L(t)=f(L(t)),\\
C_{i(L),Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0
\end{array}
\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\},
$$

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right),
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\sum_i\frac{\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}
+
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.
$$

Moreover,

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i\le(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2
\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
$$

If

$$
\tau_i=\frac{|\mathcal T_i|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_i\tau_i>0,
\qquad
\bar\delta=\frac{\sum_i\tau_i\delta_i}{\tau},
$$

then

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\frac{\delta^2/d-\delta}{p(p+1)}
+
\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$
PROOF
The exceptional graph-line bound gives

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}.
$$

An exceptional line has $p$ parameter values and therefore contributes at most $p$ accepted incidences. On a nonexceptional line, acceptance identifies $f(L(t))$ with $P_L(t)$, so the finite polar-intersection bound limits the ramified accepted incidences, with multiplicity, by $\gamma_i$. Thus

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right).
$$

Bounding $e_i$ by $\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}$, discarding the nonpositive terms $-e_i\gamma_i$, and dividing by $p^2(p+1)$ gives the normalized bound.

Formal differentiation with respect to $Z$ lowers weighted degree by at least $d$, so

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d.
$$

Substitution into $\Xi_i$ gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Xi_i
&\le(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i(\delta_i-d)-dr_i(r_i-1)\\
&=(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2\\
&=\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i-
\frac{(\delta_i-dr_i)^2}{d}\\
&\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
\end{aligned}
$$

Weighted degree is additive under products, so

$$
\sum_i\delta_i=\delta,
\qquad
\sum_i\delta_i^2\le\delta^2.
$$

It follows that

$$
\sum_i\Xi_i\le\frac{\delta^2}{d}-\delta.
$$

Finally,

$$
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}
=\frac1p\sum_i\tau_i\gamma_i
\le\frac1p\sum_i\tau_i(\delta_i-d)
=\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$

Combining these estimates yields the coarse bound.
STATUS proved

ID researcher-0007:P6.1
Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P5.1, and fix $i$. Let $G=(\mathcal X,T',E')$ be a connected bipartite graph with $\mathcal X\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2$, $T'\subseteq\mathcal T_i$, and

$$
E'\subseteq
\{(x,L)\in\mathcal X\times T':x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose $G$ has minimum degree at least the integer $k$, and suppose

$$
C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0
\qquad((x,L)\in E').
$$

If

$$
k>\delta_i
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}\le\frac{k}{4p},
$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
Q(x)=f(x)
\qquad(x\in\mathcal X)
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\{x\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\}|}{p^2}
\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$
PROOF
For every edge $(x,L)\in E'$, the assigned graph identity and acceptance imply

$$
C_i(x,f(x))=0.
$$

The cleaned-edge hypothesis gives $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0$, so this is a simple $Z$-root. Choose $b\in\mathcal X$. At least $k>\delta_i$ graph lines of the component pass through $b$. The simple-root Newton recursion from KTZ Lemmas 2.7 and 6.1, applied with weighted degree $\delta_i$, constructs a polynomial $Q$ of total degree at most $d$, agrees with every incident line label, and satisfies

$$
C_i(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0.
$$

The recursion divides only by the certified nonzero scalar $C_{i,Z}(b,f(b))$. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same $Q$ throughout the connected component, yielding $Q(x)=f(x)$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$.

For the density bound, put

$$
a=\frac{|\mathcal X|}{p^2},
\qquad
b_0=\frac{|T'|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\rho=\frac{|E'|}{p^2(p+1)},
$$

and

$$
\kappa=\frac{k}{p},
\qquad
u=\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}.
$$

Minimum degree and affine incidence mixing give

$$
\rho\ge\frac{\kappa a}{2},
\qquad
\rho\ge\kappa b_0,
\qquad
\rho\le ab_0+\nu\sqrt{ab_0}.
$$

The mixing hypothesis is $\nu\le\kappa/4$. If $a<\kappa/4$, the second and third inequalities imply $b_0<a/9$; substituting this into the upper bound contradicts $\rho\ge\kappa a/2$. If $b_0<\kappa/4$, the first and third inequalities imply $a<b_0$; substitution contradicts $\rho\ge\kappa b_0$. Therefore

$$
a,b_0\ge\frac\kappa4.
$$

Since $Q=f$ on $\mathcal X$, its global agreement is at least $a\ge k/(4p)$.
STATUS conditional

VERDICT reject
EXACT_SHA 
SUMMARY Reject the exact SHA-identified claim. Its weighted-resultant, vertical-separability, and cleanup arguments are correct under the fuller hypotheses in note Theorem 2, and the KTZ application remains at the benchmark exponent 1/3 with a valid route to one global polynomial. But the exact hashed theorem omits the hypotheses forcing Z-dependence and is concretely false at p=103,d=D=101. Because d/p<1, no exponent greater than 1/3 is obtained. P9 also needs a fixed-density quantifier, and P8 should spell out the adapted pruning step.
FATAL The exact SHA-identified theorem is false because it omits the hypotheses forcing a Z-dependent interpolant. Let p=103 and d=D=101. Choose 101 horizontal lines Y=a, label one by t^101 and the others by 0. Every weighted-degree-at-most-101 interpolant has form lambda Z+b(X,Y). Comparing the t^101 coefficients on differently labelled lines forces lambda=0. Minimum interpolants are therefore scalar multiples of the squarefree polynomial H(Y)=prod_a(Y-a), of exact weighted degree D. Removing its Z-content gives B=1. All 101 graph lines are H-trivial, whereas the hashed claim bounds their number by floor(D^2/d-D)=0 and its cleanup loss by 0.
UNFIXABLE true

The exact hashed theorem cannot be accepted. A new theorem statement and therefore a new SHA must restore n>D, N_d(D)>n(D+1), the graph identities, and A_Z≠0/Z-dependence, or explicitly assume a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant before asserting the cleanup bounds.

Correct the malformed fraction at note line 200 and narrow the claimed necessity of D<p to the actual condition deg_Z(B)<p.
Correct the exact KTZ/HKSS dependency map and identify the authoritative Revision 1 artifact rather than the cached base KTZ PDF.

ID researcher-0001:P2.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $G_0$ have the properties in P1 with line set $\mathcal L_0$. Assume $\delta^3\ge10^{15}d\sigma/p$. There is a set $R\subseteq\mathcal L_0$ such that every $L\in\mathcal L_0$ has at least half of its point-neighbors in $G_0$ also incident in $G_0$ to a line in $R\setminus\{L\}$ and, for $r=|R|$, $$100(p+1)\le r\le\frac{6400\sigma p}{\delta}.$$
PROOF
The hypotheses $\sigma\ge\delta$ and $\delta^3\ge10^{15}d\sigma/p$ imply $$\delta^2\ge\frac{10^{15}d}{p},\qquad \delta p\ge\sqrt{10^{15}dp}>3\cdot10^9,$$ and therefore $k_0\ge\delta p/200$. Select every line of $\mathcal L_0$ independently with probability $\theta=8/k_0$. For a fixed $L\in\mathcal L_0$ and a point-neighbor $x$ of $L$, at least $k_0-1$ other retained lines pass through $x$. Thus the probability that $x$ is not covered is at most $(1-\theta)^{k_0-1}$, and its coverage probability exceeds $3/4$. The coverage indicators at distinct neighbors of $L$ depend on disjoint sets of other lines. Hoeffding's inequality therefore bounds the probability that fewer than half of the neighbors are covered by $\exp(-k_0/8)$. Since $k_0\ge8\log(4p(p+1))$, a union bound over the $p(p+1)$ affine lines gives total coverage-failure probability below $1/4$. If $r=|R|$, then $$\mathbb E[r]=\theta|\mathcal L_0|\ge200(p+1),\qquad \operatorname{Var}(r)\le\mathbb E[r].$$ Chebyshev's inequality gives, with failure probability below $1/4$, $$\frac12\mathbb E[r]\le r\le2\mathbb E[r].$$ Consequently, some deterministic selection satisfies the coverage property and $$100(p+1)\le r\le\frac{16|\mathcal L_0|}{k_0}\le\frac{6400\sigma p}{\delta}.$$ These estimates incur only constant failure probability and no additional power of $\delta$.

ID researcher-0008:P4.1
For the table constructed in P3, write $\mathcal L$ for the affine lines, $A(x,L)\in\{0,1\}$ for the acceptance indicator,

$$N_E(L)=\{x\in L:A(x,L)=1\},\qquad a_x=\sum_{L\ni x}A(x,L).$$

Let $0<\theta\le1$, let $\lambda=(\lambda_M)_{M\in\mathcal L}$ be nonnegative line weights, and define

$$u_{x,L}(\lambda)=\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ni x\\M\ne L}}A(x,M)\lambda_M\right\}$$

for every accepted incidence $(x,L)$. If

$$\sum_{x\in N_E(L)}u_{x,L}(\lambda)\ge\theta\lvert N_E(L)\rvert$$

for every $L\in\mathcal L$, then

$$\sum_{M\in\mathcal L}\lambda_M\ge\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}.$$

The same conclusion holds when every $\lambda_M$ belongs to $\{0,1\}$.
PROOF
Define the larger self-inclusive capped load

$$v_x=\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\in\mathcal L\\x\in M}}A(x,M)\lambda_M\right\}.$$

For every accepted incidence $(x,L)$, one has $u_{x,L}(\lambda)\le v_x$. Since every line has $d$ accepted points, there are $p(p+1)$ lines, and $a_x\le d+1$, double counting gives

$$\theta d\,p(p+1)\le\sum_{L\in\mathcal L}\sum_{x\in N_E(L)}u_{x,L}(\lambda)\le\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}a_xv_x\le(d+1)\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}v_x.$$

Nonnegativity of the weights gives

$$\sum_xv_x\le\sum_x\sum_{\substack{M\in\mathcal L\\x\in M}}A(x,M)\lambda_M=\sum_{M\in\mathcal L}\lvert N_E(M)\rvert\lambda_M=d\sum_{M\in\mathcal L}\lambda_M.$$

Combining the two displays and dividing by $d>0$ yields the claimed lower bound. No Markov inequality, union bound, or Cauchy–Schwarz inequality is used. Indicator weights are ordinary selected-line families, so the same bound applies to that special case and to the stricter other-line reaching condition.

ID researcher-0008:P5.1
For the table constructed in P3 and every $0<\theta\le1$, assign every affine line the weight

$$\lambda_M=\frac{\theta}{d-1}.$$

These weights are $\theta$-reaching through other accepted lines and have total weight

$$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

If $\operatorname{OPT}_\theta$ is the infimum of the total weight over all nonnegative $\theta$-reaching weights, then

$$\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1},$$

and the multiplicative gap between this upper bound and the lower-bound value $\theta p(p+1)/(d+1)$ is

$$\frac{d+1}{d-1}\le\frac{102}{100}.$$
PROOF
At every accepted incidence $(x,L)$, the number of other accepted lines through $x$ is

$$a_x-1\ge d-1.$$

For the uniform weights,

$$\sum_{\substack{M\ni x\\M\ne L}}A(x,M)\lambda_M\ge(d-1)\frac{\theta}{d-1}=\theta.$$

Because $\theta\le1$, capping the load at $1$ still gives $u_{x,L}(\lambda)\ge\theta$. Hence the weights are $\theta$-reaching. Since $\lvert\mathcal L\rvert=p(p+1)$, their total weight is

$$\sum_{M\in\mathcal L}\lambda_M=p(p+1)\frac{\theta}{d-1}.$$

The ratio of this value to the lower-bound value $\theta p(p+1)/(d+1)$ is $(d+1)/(d-1)$. Since $d\ge101$,

$$\frac{d+1}{d-1}\le\frac{102}{100}.$$

ID researcher-0008:P6.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and consider any valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ with line-label degree at most $d$. Let $A(x,L)$ be its acceptance indicator, let

$$\epsilon=\frac{\sum_{x,L\ni x}A(x,L)}{p^2(p+1)},\qquad a_x=\sum_{L\ni x}A(x,L),$$

and let $\Gamma_2$ be the probability that a uniformly chosen ordered pair of distinct lines through a uniformly chosen point are both accepted there. Then

$$\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}p+\frac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$
PROOF
At a fixed point $x$, exactly $a_x(a_x-1)$ ordered pairs of distinct incident lines are jointly accepted. Since there are $p(p+1)$ ordered pairs of distinct lines through $x$,

$$\Gamma_2=\frac{\mathbb E_x[a_x(a_x-1)]}{p(p+1)}.$$

The mean accepted degree is

$$\mathbb E_x[a_x]=(p+1)\epsilon.$$

Using $\mathbb E_x[a_x^2]=(\mathbb E_x[a_x])^2+\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)$ gives

$$\Gamma_2=\frac{(p+1)^2\epsilon^2-(p+1)\epsilon+\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)},$$

which simplifies to the asserted identity. Nonnegativity of the variance gives

$$\Gamma_2\ge\epsilon^2-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}p.$$

If $\epsilon\ge2/p$, then $\epsilon(1-\epsilon)/p\le\epsilon/p\le\epsilon^2/2$, and therefore $\Gamma_2\ge\epsilon^2/2$. This is the exact variance, or Jensen, loss; no further concentration loss occurs.

ID researcher-0008:P9.1
Fix $C>0$ and $\alpha>1/2$, set $d=101$, and let $p>101$ tend to infinity through primes. If

$$\epsilon=C\left(\frac{101}{p}\right)^\alpha,$$

then $\epsilon\sqrt p\to0$, and the absorption condition

$$(p+1)^{-1/2}\le\frac{\epsilon}{8000}$$

fails for all sufficiently large $p$. The KTZ/Vinh component-recovery step does not yield its linear global-agreement conclusion in this regime.
PROOF
KTZ Lemma 2.1 gives the absolute affine form of Vinh's spectral estimate with normalized error $(p+1)^{-1/2}$. KTZ Lemma 6.2 absorbs that error only under

$$(p+1)^{-1/2}\le\frac{\epsilon}{8000}.$$

At the cubic threshold the condition is weaker, since

$$\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3}\sqrt p=d^{1/3}p^{1/6}.$$

For the parameters in the statement,

$$\epsilon\sqrt p=C\,101^\alpha p^{1/2-\alpha}\longrightarrow0.$$

Thus the absorption inequality fails for all sufficiently large $p$. This is an obstruction to the Vinh-only recovery method, not a counterexample to a stronger soundness theorem.

Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d\le p-2$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, and set $D=d+1$, $s=\lfloor(d+1)/2\rfloor$, and $e=d+1-2s\in\{0,1\}$. Choose distinct $c_1,\ldots,c_s\in\mathbb F_p$. If $e=1$, choose $b\in\mathbb F_p\setminus\{c_1,\ldots,c_s\}$, and define $H(Y)=\prod_{j=1}^s(Y-c_j)^2(Y-b)^e$ and $B(X,Y,Z)=XZ+H(Y)$, with the last factor omitted when $e=0$. Then $B$ is primitive in $Z$, squarefree, and absolutely irreducible, with $\operatorname{wdeg}B=D<p$, $B_Z=X$, and $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. Its normalized degree-one discriminant is $1$, while $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=X$, whose zero set is a smooth absolutely irreducible affine line. For every $1\le j\le s$, the point $(0,c_j,0)$ is singular on $B=0$.

IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH [definition/candidate]
DEPS 
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and polynomials $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ for all affine lines $L$. Define $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(L(t),L):P_L(t)=f(L(t))\},\qquad \epsilon=\frac{|E_{\rm acc}|}{p^2(p+1)},$$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$ put $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\quad \mu(G)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\quad \tau(G)=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},\quad k(G)=\operatorname{mindeg}(G).$$
NOTES
All measures use uniform affine point-line incidence normalization; the scope is only prime $p$ and $100<d<p$.

IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
For $d\le D$, let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},\qquad N_d(D)=\dim V_D,$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ define $$N_G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E\},$$ $$\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)=\{x\in N_G(L):\exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ x\in N_G(M)\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
NOTES
The definition measures labeled constraint rank rather than the cardinality of the sampled family.

IE-L-POPULAR-CORE [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE researcher-0001:P1:c840bab57ab5b1826bf693fdd043de875d845c01777c77832564af0183e300ff
STATEMENT
If an accepted carrier $G$ has $e(G)=\delta>0$ and $\tau(G)\le\sigma$, then, for $k_0=\lfloor\delta p/100\rfloor$, it contains $G_0$ with $$e(G_0)\ge\delta/4,\qquad k(G_0)\ge k_0,\qquad \delta/4\le\tau(G_0)\le\sigma.$$
NOTES
The formal conclusion retains constant mass; its proof actually gives a smaller deletion loss. The source audit verdict is `revise`, so the harness determines status.

IE-L-COMP-MEASURE [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE researcher-0002:P1:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f
STATEMENT
Let $G$ be a connected accepted graph with minimum degree $k$, line measure $\tau$, and $C_*=2^{40}$. If $$k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\mu(G)\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.$$
NOTES
This is the exact component-measure baseline for the cardinality sampler.

IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-COMP-MEASURE
EVIDENCE researcher-0002:P2:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f
STATEMENT
Under `IE-L-COMP-MEASURE`, writing $t=|T|$, there exists $R\subseteq T$ such that $$\frac{4t}{k}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{k}$$ and $$|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\frac12\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\in T$. Hence $|R|=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$.
NOTES
The sample loses only absolute constants but its size remains governed by line support.

IE-T-COMP-DECODE [theorem/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE
EVIDENCE researcher-0002:P3:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f; researcher-0002:P4:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f; researcher-0002:P5:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f; researcher-0002:P6:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f; researcher-0002:P7:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f; researcher-0002:P8:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f
STATEMENT
If a connected accepted graph of minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\tau$ satisfies $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
NOTES
This compresses the accepted interpolation, extension, cleanup, and component recovery chain.

IE-T-SQRT-MILESTONE [theorem/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-POPULAR-CORE, IE-T-COMP-DECODE
EVIDENCE researcher-0002:P9:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f
STATEMENT
Let $\Lambda\ge1$, and let $G_0$ be the popularity core with minimum degree $\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. If some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\Lambda\epsilon$ and $$\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\cdot2^{40}\Lambda}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/2},$$ then one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.$$
NOTES
The required small-line-measure component remains an antecedent, not a proved localization theorem.

IE-R-SQRT-CEILING [reduction/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-COMP-MEASURE
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
If $k\le C\epsilon p$, $\tau\ge k/(4p)$, and $k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2$, then $$\epsilon^2\ge\frac{C_*}{4C^2}\frac dp.$$
NOTES
Thus the accepted cardinality/root-budget architecture cannot reach exponent $1-o(1)$ merely by finding smaller components.

IE-L-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE researcher-0002:P10:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f; researcher-0002:P11:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f
STATEMENT
For $p\ge103$ and $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$ there is a legal table with $$\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K$$ such that every $R$ reaching half of every accepted line satisfies $$|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon},$$ while some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
NOTES
This obstructs graph-only sparsification, not soundness, because the same table has an immediate decoder.

IE-L-FRACTIONAL-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE researcher-0008:P1:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd; researcher-0008:P2:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd; researcher-0008:P3:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd; researcher-0008:P4:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd; researcher-0008:P5:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd
STATEMENT
There is a legal full-line-support table with $\epsilon=d/p$, every line of accepted degree $d$, every point of accepted degree $d$ or $d+1$, and the global decoder $Q=0$. For weights $\lambda_M\ge0$, require for every line $L$ that $$\sum_{x\in N(L)}\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ne L\\x\in N(M)}}\lambda_M\right\}\ge\frac d2.$$ The minimum total weight $W=\sum_M\lambda_M$ satisfies $$\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d+1)}\le W\le\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d-1)}=\Theta\left(\frac p\epsilon\right).$$
NOTES
The source audit verdict is `revise`; this node is diagnostic and lies outside the goal ancestry.

IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE researcher-0008:P6:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd; researcher-0008:P7:bb3beec49631e8d11ccc6b08655db7b8adf908213210af833dd95a78087710bd
STATEMENT
Let $a_x=|\{L:(x,L)\in E_{\rm acc}\}|$ and let $$\Gamma_2=\frac{1}{p^3(p+1)}\sum_x a_x(a_x-1).$$ Then $$\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}p+\frac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$ In particular, $\Gamma_2\ge\epsilon^2/2$ when $\epsilon\ge2/p$, and equality in the variance-free formula is attained by accepting $k$ direction classes, where $\epsilon=k/(p+1)$ and $Q=0$.
NOTES
This identifies degree variance as the only correction to the random two-line baseline; the source audit verdict is `revise`.

IE-X-EXACT-LINEAR [theorem/refuted]
DEPS IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
EVIDENCE researcher-0001:P13:c840bab57ab5b1826bf693fdd043de875d845c01777c77832564af0183e300ff; researcher-0001:P14:c840bab57ab5b1826bf693fdd043de875d845c01777c77832564af0183e300ff
STATEMENT
The assertion that there exist absolute $C,c>0$ such that every legal instance satisfies $$\epsilon\ge C\frac dp\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c\epsilon$$ is false, already for $d=101$.
NOTES
The modal construction refutes this uniform endpoint but remains below every fixed $(d/p)^{1-\eta}$ trigger.

IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY [obligation/open]
DEPS IE-D-RANK-REACH, IE-L-POPULAR-CORE
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$ there exist $C_\theta\ge1$, $0<a_\theta\le1$, and $K_\theta\ge1$ such that, if $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta},$$ then either $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,$$ or there are a popularity core $G_0$, a line set $T\subseteq T(G_0)$, and $G=G_0[T]$ retaining every core edge incident with $T$, together with $R$ and $D$, such that $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,
\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d,$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.
NOTES
This is the principal incidence obligation. The direct-decoder alternative is essential by both cover obstructions.

IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE [reduction/candidate]
DEPS IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
After enlarging $C_\theta$ so that $C_\theta\ge16K_\theta/a_\theta$, the structured branch satisfies $$\frac{D}{\epsilon p}\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\le\frac{a_\theta}{16},\qquad D\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon p}{16}<p.$$ Indeed, with $\rho=d/p<1$, one has $\rho=\rho^{1-\theta}\rho^\theta\le\epsilon/C_\theta$.
NOTES
The constant $C_\theta$ may be enlarged without weakening the dichotomy; no characteristic-size hypothesis beyond $d<p$ is introduced.

IE-L-RANK-INTERP [lemma/drafting]
DEPS IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
In the structured branch there exists a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0$$ for every $M\in R$.
NOTES
A kernel element is replaced by its squarefree radical. More than $D$ distinct sampled lines exclude a $Z$-independent radical.

IE-L-RANK-EXTEND [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-RANK-INTERP
EVIDENCE researcher-0002:P4:f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f
STATEMENT
For every line vertex $L$ of $G$, the $D+1$ distinct reached accepted points force $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
NOTES
Only the accepted root-counting implication is imported; the rank-producing construction is supplied upstream.

IE-L-PRIMITIVE-SEPARABLE [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-RANK-EXTEND
EVIDENCE researcher-0006:P1:b242e553383c9f56cd665197bb10990aef5a2ee876f0d378174920fc4ec25aef
STATEMENT
Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content, $B$ is primitive in $Z$, $h=\deg H$, and $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(B)$. Then $B$ is squarefree and $Z$-dependent, $$h+\delta\le D,$$ and $$\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$
NOTES
The gate gives $\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<p<dp$, within the exact prime-field separability range.

IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION [lemma/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-PRIMITIVE-SEPARABLE
EVIDENCE researcher-0006:P2:b242e553383c9f56cd665197bb10990aef5a2ee876f0d378174920fc4ec25aef
STATEMENT
If coprime weighted polynomials $U,V$ have weights at most $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, then they support at most $su+rv-drs$ common degree-$d$ graph lines. Consequently, if $r=\deg_ZB$, the number of graph lines satisfying both $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$$ is at most $$(2r-1)\delta-dr^2\le\frac{\delta^2}{d}-\delta.$$
NOTES
This is the accepted weighted-resultant estimate applied to $B$ and $B_Z$.

IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT [lemma/drafting]
DEPS IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
At most $$h+\frac{\delta^2}{d}-\delta\le\frac{D^2}{d}-D$$ lines are content-trivial or identically $B_Z$-ramified. On every other line, $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ and the nonzero polynomial $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))$ has at most $\delta-d\le D-d$ roots. Hence deleting all nonsimple accepted incidences costs at most $$\frac{D^2/d-D}{p(p+1)}+\frac{D-d}{p}\le\left(\frac Dp\right)^2+rac Dp.$$
NOTES
This sharpens the round-1 generic $pD^2+D|T|$ count and avoids directional-derivative transfer.

IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN [reduction/drafting]
DEPS IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
After the vertical deletion, iteratively delete every point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. Peeling costs less than $$\frac{D(|\mathbb F_p^2|+|\mathcal L_p|)}{p^2(p+1)}<\frac{2D}{p}.$$ The gate therefore leaves a nonempty graph $G_1$ satisfying $$e(G_1)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2}\epsilon,
\qquad k(G_1)>D,$$ with $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line and $$B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$$ at every point vertex.
NOTES
Each edge removed by peeling is charged once to the vertex deleted at that stage.

IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE [lemma/drafting]
DEPS IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
Factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and put $\Delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$. Primitivity makes every $C_i$ depend on $Z$, so $$s\le\deg_ZB\le\frac Dd\le K_\theta.$$ The simple-root graph is a disjoint union of factor classes $F_i$, each preserving all degrees of its vertices. For some $i$, $$e(F_i)\ge\frac{e(G_1)}s\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon,
\qquad k(F_i)>D\ge\Delta_i,$$ and $$C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0$$ on its line and point vertices, respectively.
NOTES
Uniqueness at simple lifted points makes every star monochromatic, so factor selection preserves rather than averages vertex degrees.

IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT [obligation/drafting]
DEPS IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)\le D<p$. If a nonempty accepted factor class $F$ has $k(F)>D$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\sim Z-Q.$$
NOTES
A fresh exact audit is required. Researcher-0003 P7 is audit-inexact, while researcher-0007 P6 is conditional and does not state this cross-component association.

IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE [reduction/candidate]
DEPS IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
For the factor class $F$ from `IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE`, $C\sim Z-Q$ implies $Q(x)=f(x)$ at every point vertex of $F$. Since every affine point lies on at most $p+1$ lines, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X(F)|}{p^2}\ge e(F)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$
NOTES
Association to one fixed factor aggregates all disconnected factor components without Vinh mixing.

IE-R-CLOSE [reduction/blocked]
DEPS IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
For arbitrary fixed $\eta>0$, set $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Since $0<d/p<1$ and $$(d/p)^{1-\eta}\ge(d/p)^{1-\theta},$$ the dichotomy and factor aggregation yield constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES
The direct branch is already terminal; the structured branch uses only a constant number of factors and loses no power of $d/p$.

IE-GOAL [theorem/blocked]
DEPS IE-R-CLOSE
EVIDENCE 
STATEMENT
For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, integer $100<d<p$, function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES
Blocked on the bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy and a fresh exact factor-lift audit. Work states do not assert proof verification.

ID researcher-0006:P1.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in $Z$, and satisfies $\operatorname{wdeg}B<dp$, then $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.
PROOF
Put $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}B$. Then $\deg_ZB\le\lfloor\delta/d\rfloor<p$. Suppose an irreducible polynomial $C$ divides both $B$ and $B_Z$. Writing $B=CE$, squarefreeness gives $C\nmid E$, while differentiation modulo $C$ gives $C\mid C_ZE$. Hence $C\mid C_Z$, so $C_Z=0$. In characteristic $p$, every positive $Z$-exponent of $C$ is therefore divisible by $p$. Since $\deg_ZC<p$, the polynomial $C$ is independent of $Z$, contradicting the primitivity of $B$. Thus $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.

The strict threshold is sharp: $Z^p-X$ is primitive, squarefree, smooth, and absolutely irreducible, has weighted degree $dp$, and has zero $Z$-derivative.

ID researcher-0006:P2.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with $u=\operatorname{wdeg}U$, $v=\operatorname{wdeg}V$, $r=\deg_ZU$, and $s=\deg_ZV$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines with parametrizations $L(t)$ and labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ such that $U(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ for every $L\in\mathcal T$. Then $|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs$.
PROOF
Write $U=\sum_iU_iZ^i$ and $V=\sum_jV_jZ^j$. The weighted-degree constraints give $\deg U_i\le u-di$ and $\deg V_j\le v-dj$. The nonzero resultant $R=\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)$ is homogeneous of degree $s$ in the coefficients of $U$ and degree $r$ in those of $V$. Every resultant monomial has total vertical-index weight $rs$, so its ordinary $(X,Y)$-degree is at most $su+rv-drs$. Hence $\deg R\le su+rv-drs$.

For each $L\in\mathcal T$, the specialized polynomials $U(L(t),Z)$ and $V(L(t),Z)$ have the common factor $Z-P_L(t)$, so $R(L(t))\equiv0$. The affine linear equation of $L$ therefore divides $R$. Distinct lines give distinct factors, proving the bound. No leading coefficient is divided out.

        return {"schema": "line-point-roadmap-message-board-v1", "messages": []}

    def _prompt(
            self, definition: dict[str, str], snapshot: dict[str, Any],
            round_number: int) -> str:
        current = self._current(definition["id"])
        board = self._message_board()
        return f"""You are the proof-roadmap agent for `{definition['id']}` in the bivariate
prime-field line-versus-point campaign. Work at the level of a Lean-style proof plan: give a
concise but complete directed acyclic chain of definitions, reductions, lemmas, obligations, and
the final theorem. Your focus is {definition['focus']}.

All work is shared. Read TARGET.md, the complete submissions, verifier audits, Lemma Book,
all roadmaps under {self.root}, and the informal message board at
{self.root / 'message-board.json'}. You may cite and reuse lemmas from any source or roadmap.
Treat message-board claims as informal suggestions, never as verified mathematics. Give roughly
60 percent of your attention to your assigned focus so the three agents explore distinct routes,
but import any useful shared lemma. Cite it in evidence_refs by the exact stable triple
(source_job_id, source_step_id, source_response_sha256) from corpus-index.json. Editorial lemma
splits are display-only and never change this proof reference.

The scope is fixed at $m=2$ over prime $\\mathbb F_p$ with integer $100<d<p$. Do not work on
dimension bootstrapping or $d\\le100$. The target is soundness $(d/p)^{{1-o(1)}}$ with recovery of
one global total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial. Use work_state only to report activity: `open`,
`drafting`, `candidate`, `blocked`, or `refuted`. The harness—not you—derives proof status from
exact source hashes and independent verifier audits. Never convert confidence, a polished lemma,
or informal discussion into verified proof progress.

This is roadmap round {round_number}. The shared corpus snapshot SHA-256 is
{snapshot['sha256']}. Set those exact values in the response. Preserve useful nodes from your
previous roadmap while making dependencies more complete and concise. Every dependency must
refer to another node in this roadmap. The critical_path must list the shortest dependency chain
ending at goal_node_id. Keep node statements mathematical and minimal; put short planning context
in notes. Enclose math in $...$ or $$...$$ using KaTeX-supported LaTeX.

Post up to four informal messages for the other roadmap agents: questions, warnings, proposed
lemma imports, or requests for help. Messages may be candid and exploratory, but must cite the
relevant roadmap/node/lemma ids when possible. Use channel `global` or one of the three roadmap
ids. Use in_reply_to only for an id already present on the shared board. related_node_ids may
contain only nodes in this submitted roadmap.

ASSIGNED ROADMAP:
{json.dumps(definition, indent=2, sort_keys=True)}

PREVIOUS VERSION OF THIS ROADMAP:
{json.dumps(current, indent=2, sort_keys=True) if current else 'None'}

CURRENT SHARED MESSAGE BOARD:
{json.dumps(board, indent=2, sort_keys=True)}

CORPUS RECEIPT:
{json.dumps(snapshot['files'], indent=2, sort_keys=True)}

STABLE PROOF-EVIDENCE INDEX:
{json.dumps(snapshot['evidence'], indent=2, sort_keys=True)}
"""

    def _node_executable(self) -> str:
        node = shutil.which("node")
        if node:
            return node
        pnpm = shutil.which("pnpm")
        bundled = (Path(pnpm).resolve().parents[2] / "node" / "bin" / "node") if pnpm else None
        if bundled and bundled.exists():
            return str(bundled)
        raise AgentError("node executable not found for deterministic KaTeX validation")

    def _render_check(self, response: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
        script = self.paths.workspace / "dashboard" / "scripts" / "validate-roadmap-math.mjs"
        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile("w", suffix=".json", delete=False) as handle:
            json.dump(response, handle)
            temporary = Path(handle.name)
        try:
            completed = subprocess.run(
                [self._node_executable(), str(script), str(temporary)],
                cwd=self.paths.workspace / "dashboard",
                text=True,
                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
                timeout=120,
                check=False,
            )
        finally:
            temporary.unlink(missing_ok=True)
        if completed.returncode != 0:
            raise AgentError(
                "roadmap KaTeX validation failed: " +
                (completed.stderr or completed.stdout).strip())
        return json.loads(completed.stdout)

    def _run_agent(
            self, definition: dict[str, str], snapshot: dict[str, Any],
            round_number: int) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]]:
        errors: list[str] = []
        max_attempts = int(self.cfg.get("roadmap_max_attempts", 4))
        for _ in range(max_attempts):
            prompt = self._prompt(definition, snapshot, round_number)
            if errors:
                prompt += "\n\nThe previous attempt was rejected for:\n- " + "\n- ".join(errors)
            response, metadata = self.provider.run(
                f"roadmap-{definition['id']}-round-{round_number:04d}",
                prompt,
                ROADMAP_SCHEMA,
            )
            errors = validate_roadmap_response(
                definition, snapshot["sha256"], round_number, response,
                evidence_index=snapshot["evidence"],
                known_message_ids={
                    str(message.get("id"))
                    for message in self._message_board().get("messages", [])
                },
            )
            if errors:
                continue
            try:
                render_validation = self._render_check(response)
            except AgentError as exc:
                errors = [str(exc)]
                continue
            response["render_validation"] = render_validation
            return response, metadata
        raise AgentError(
            f"roadmap agent exhausted {max_attempts} attempts for {definition['id']}: " +
            "; ".join(errors))

    @staticmethod
    def _derive_progress(
            nodes: list[dict[str, Any]], goal_node_id: str,
            evidence_index: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, int]]:
        node_map = {str(node["id"]): node for node in nodes}
        base: dict[str, str] = {}

import fs from 'node:fs';
import katex from 'katex';

const path = process.argv[2] ?? 'public/research-data.json';
const data = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8'));
const roadmaps = data.proof_roadmaps?.roadmaps ?? (data.nodes ? [data] : []);
const messages = data.message_board?.messages ?? data.messages ?? [];
const failures = [];
let mathSegments = 0;

function validateMarkdown(markdown, reference) {
  const value = String(markdown ?? '');
  const control = Array.from(value).find((character) => {
    const code = character.charCodeAt(0);
    return code < 32 && character !== '\n' && character !== '\t';
  });
  if (control) failures.push(`${reference}: contains a control character`);
  const mathPattern = /\$\$([\s\S]*?)\$\$|\$([^$\n]+?)\$/g;
  const prose = value.replace(mathPattern, (_, display, inline) => {
    const expression = display ?? inline ?? '';
    mathSegments += 1;
    try {
      katex.renderToString(expression, { throwOnError: true, strict: 'warn' });
    } catch (error) {
      failures.push(`${reference}: ${error.message}`);
    }
    return ' ';
  });
  if ((value.match(/\$/g) ?? []).length % 2 !== 0) {
    failures.push(`${reference}: unbalanced math delimiter`);
  }
  if (/\\[A-Za-z]+/.test(prose)) {
    failures.push(`${reference}: TeX command outside math delimiters`);
  }
}

for (const roadmap of roadmaps) {
  validateMarkdown(roadmap.target_statement, `${roadmap.roadmap_id} target`);
  for (const node of roadmap.nodes ?? []) {
    validateMarkdown(node.statement_markdown, `${roadmap.roadmap_id}:${node.id}`);
  }
}
for (const message of messages) {
  validateMarkdown(message.body_markdown, `${message.id ?? 'draft-message'} body`);
}

if (failures.length) {
  process.stderr.write(`${failures.join('\n')}\n`);
  process.exit(1);
}
process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify({ passed: true, roadmaps: roadmaps.length, messages: messages.length, math_segments: mathSegments })}\n`);

def file_sha256(path: Path) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()


def validate_roadmap_response(
        definition: dict[str, str], corpus_sha: str, round_number: int,
        response: dict[str, Any], evidence_index: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
        known_message_ids: set[str] | None = None) -> list[str]:
    errors: list[str] = []
    if response.get("roadmap_id") != definition["id"]:
        errors.append("roadmap_id does not match the assigned roadmap")
    if response.get("corpus_sha256") != corpus_sha:
        errors.append("corpus_sha256 does not match the assigned snapshot")
    if response.get("round") != round_number:
        errors.append("round does not match the assigned round")
    nodes = response.get("nodes", [])
    if not nodes:
        errors.append("roadmap contains no dependency nodes")
        return errors

 succeeded in 0ms:
def validate_roadmap_response(
        definition: dict[str, str], corpus_sha: str, round_number: int,
        response: dict[str, Any], evidence_index: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
        known_message_ids: set[str] | None = None) -> list[str]:
    errors: list[str] = []
    if response.get("roadmap_id") != definition["id"]:
        errors.append("roadmap_id does not match the assigned roadmap")
    if response.get("corpus_sha256") != corpus_sha:
        errors.append("corpus_sha256 does not match the assigned snapshot")
    if response.get("round") != round_number:
        errors.append("round does not match the assigned round")
    nodes = response.get("nodes", [])
    if not nodes:
        errors.append("roadmap contains no dependency nodes")
        return errors
    node_ids = [str(node.get("id", "")) for node in nodes]
    if any(not node_id for node_id in node_ids) or len(set(node_ids)) != len(node_ids):
        errors.append("roadmap node ids must be nonempty and unique")
    known = set(node_ids)
    for node in nodes:
        node_id = str(node.get("id", ""))
        unknown = sorted(set(node.get("dependencies", [])) - known)
        if unknown:
            errors.append(f"{node_id} has unknown dependencies: {', '.join(unknown)}")
        if not str(node.get("statement_markdown", "")).strip():
            errors.append(f"node {node_id} has an empty statement")
        for reference in node.get("evidence_refs", []):
            key = "|".join(str(reference.get(field, "")) for field in (
                "source_job_id", "source_step_id", "source_response_sha256"))
            if evidence_index is not None and key not in evidence_index:
                errors.append(f"{node_id} cites unknown or stale evidence: {key}")
    goal = str(response.get("goal_node_id", ""))
    if goal not in known:
        errors.append("goal_node_id is not a roadmap node")
    critical = response.get("critical_path", [])
    if not critical or any(node_id not in known for node_id in critical):
        errors.append("critical_path must be a nonempty list of known nodes")
    elif critical[-1] != goal:
        errors.append("critical_path must end at goal_node_id")

    visiting: set[str] = set()
    visited: set[str] = set()
    dependencies = {
        str(node.get("id", "")): [str(value) for value in node.get("dependencies", [])]
        for node in nodes
    }

    def visit(node_id: str) -> None:
        if node_id in visiting:
            errors.append(f"dependency cycle detected at {node_id}")
            return
        if node_id in visited:
            return
        visiting.add(node_id)
        for dependency in dependencies.get(node_id, []):
            visit(dependency)
        visiting.remove(node_id)
        visited.add(node_id)

    for node_id in node_ids:
        visit(node_id)
    board_ids = known_message_ids or set()
    for message in response.get("messages", []):
        channel = str(message.get("channel", ""))
        if channel not in {"global", *(item["id"] for item in ROADMAP_DEFINITIONS)}:
            errors.append(f"unknown message channel: {channel}")
        reply = message.get("in_reply_to")
        if reply is not None and reply not in board_ids:
            errors.append(f"message replies to unknown post: {reply}")
        related = set(message.get("related_node_ids", []))
        if not related.issubset(known):
            errors.append("message references a node outside its submitted roadmap")
    return errors


class RoadmapWorkshop:
    """Run three focused roadmap agents over one shared mathematical corpus."""

    def __init__(self, config_path: Path | str):
        self.config, self.paths = load_campaign_config(config_path)
        self.cfg = self.config["campaign"]
        self.root = self.paths.campaign_dir / "roadmaps"
        self.runtime = self.root / "runtime"
        self.root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.runtime.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.provider = CommandAgentProvider(
            self.paths.workspace,
            self.root / "agent_logs",
            executable=str(self.cfg.get("executable", "codex")),
            model=str(self.cfg.get("model", "gpt-5.6-sol")),
            reasoning_effort=str(self.cfg.get("reasoning_effort", "ultra")),
            disable_nested_agents=True,
            timeout_seconds=int(self.cfg.get("roadmap_timeout_seconds", 5400)),
        )

    def corpus_snapshot(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        patterns = (
            "submissions/*/response.json",
            "reviews/*/*/audit.json",
            "lemma_book/submissions/*/response.json",
        )
        paths = sorted({
            path for pattern in patterns
            for path in self.paths.campaign_dir.glob(pattern)
            if path.is_file()
        })
        digest = hashlib.sha256()
        receipts = []
        for path in paths:
            relative = path.relative_to(self.paths.workspace).as_posix()
            value = file_sha256(path)
            digest.update(relative.encode())
            digest.update(value.encode())
            receipts.append({"path": relative, "sha256": value})
        evidence: dict[str, Any] = {}
        submissions_root = self.paths.campaign_dir / "submissions"
        for response_path in sorted(submissions_root.glob("*/response.json")):
            source_job_id = response_path.parent.name
            source = json.loads(response_path.read_text())
            source_sha = hashlib.sha256(
                json.dumps(source, sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest()
            claim = source.get("theorem_statement") or source.get("integrated_theorem", "")
            claim_sha = hashlib.sha256(str(claim).encode()).hexdigest()
            audit_path = (self.paths.campaign_dir / "reviews" / source_job_id /
                          f"verifier-{source_job_id}" / "audit.json")
            audit = json.loads(audit_path.read_text()) if audit_path.exists() else None
            audit_exact = bool(
                audit and audit.get("verified_claim_sha256") == claim_sha)
            lemma_path = self.paths.campaign_dir / "lemma_book" / "submissions" / source_job_id / "response.json"
            lemma_parts: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
            if lemma_path.exists():
                lemma_response = json.loads(lemma_path.read_text())
                for lemma in lemma_response.get("lemmas", []):
                    lemma_parts.setdefault(str(lemma.get("source_step_id", "")), []).append(
                        str(lemma.get("id", "")))
            for step in source.get("proof_steps", []):
                key = "|".join((source_job_id, str(step["id"]), source_sha))
                evidence[key] = {
                    "source_job_id": source_job_id,
                    "source_step_id": str(step["id"]),
                    "source_response_sha256": source_sha,
                    "source_status": step.get("status", "conditional"),
                    "audit_verdict": audit.get("verdict") if audit else "pending",
                    "audit_exact": audit_exact,
                    "lemma_ids": sorted(lemma_parts.get(str(step["id"]), [])),
                }
        payload = {"sha256": digest.hexdigest(), "files": receipts, "evidence": evidence}
        (self.root / "corpus-index.json").write_text(
            json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n")
        return payload

    def _current(self, roadmap_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        path = self.root / roadmap_id / "current.json"
        return json.loads(path.read_text()) if path.exists() else None

    def _message_board(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        path = self.root / "message-board.json"
        if path.exists():
            return json.loads(path.read_text())
        return {"schema": "line-point-roadmap-message-board-v1", "messages": []}
